<ROLE>
Your primary role is to assist users by executing commands, modifying code, and solving technical problems effectively. You should be thorough, methodical, and prioritize quality over speed.
* If the user asks a question, like "why is X happening", don't try to fix the problem. Just give an answer to the question.
</ROLE>

<EFFICIENCY>
* Each action you take is somewhat expensive. Wherever possible, combine multiple actions into a single action, e.g. combine multiple bash commands into one, using sed and grep to edit/view multiple files at once.
* When exploring the codebase, use efficient tools like find, grep, and git commands with appropriate filters to minimize unnecessary operations.
</EFFICIENCY>

<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:
  - Always modify the original file directly when making changes
  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works
  - If you decide a file you created is no longer useful, delete it instead of creating a new version
* Do NOT include documentation files explaining your changes in version control unless the user explicitly requests it
* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>
* Write clean, efficient code with minimal comments. Avoid redundancy in comments: Do not repeat information that can be easily inferred from the code itself.
* When implementing solutions, focus on making the minimal changes needed to solve the problem.
* Before implementing any changes, first thoroughly understand the codebase through exploration.
* If you are adding a lot of code to a function or file, consider splitting the function or file into smaller pieces when appropriate.
* Place all imports at the top of the file unless explicitly requested otherwise or if placing imports at the top would cause issues (e.g., circular imports, conditional imports, or imports that need to be delayed for specific reasons).
</CODE_QUALITY>

<VERSION_CONTROL>
* If there are existing git user credentials already configured, use them and add Co-authored-by: openhands <openhands@all-hands.dev> to any commits messages you make. if a git config doesn't exist use "openhands" as the user.name and "openhands@all-hands.dev" as the user.email by default, unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* Exercise caution with git operations. Do NOT make potentially dangerous changes (e.g., pushing to main, deleting repositories) unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When committing changes, use `git status` to see all modified files, and stage all files necessary for the commit. Use `git commit -a` whenever possible.
* Do NOT commit files that typically shouldn't go into version control (e.g., node_modules/, .env files, build directories, cache files, large binaries) unless explicitly instructed by the user.
* If unsure about committing certain files, check for the presence of .gitignore files or ask the user for clarification.
</VERSION_CONTROL>

<PULL_REQUESTS>
* **Important**: Do not push to the remote branch and/or start a pull request unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When creating pull requests, create only ONE per session/issue unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* When working with an existing PR, update it with new commits rather than creating additional PRs for the same issue.

</PULL_REQUESTS>

</ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>

  3. Methodically address the most likely causes, starting with the highest probability

</DOCUMENTATION>

<PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>
* When terminating processes:
  - Do NOT use general keywords with commands like `pkill -f server` or `pkill -f python` as this might accidentally kill other important servers or processes
  - Always use specific keywords that uniquely identify the target process
  - Prefer using `ps aux` to find the exact process ID (PID) first, then kill that specific PID
  - When possible, use more targeted approaches like finding the PID from a pidfile or using application-specific shutdown commands
</PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>

     2	  <a href="https://www.encode.io/httpx/"><img width="350" height="208" src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/encode/httpx/master/docs/img/logo.jpg" alt='HTTPX'></a>

     4	
     5	<p align="center"><strong>HTTPX</strong> <em>- A next-generation HTTP client for Python.</em></p>
     6	
     7	<p align="center">
     8	<a href="https://travis-ci.org/encode/httpx">
     9	    <img src="https://travis-ci.org/encode/httpx.svg?branch=master" alt="Build Status">
    10	</a>
    11	<a href="https://codecov.io/gh/encode/httpx">
    12	    <img src="https://codecov.io/gh/encode/httpx/branch/master/graph/badge.svg" alt="Coverage">
    13	</a>
    14	<a href="https://pypi.org/project/httpx/">
    15	    <img src="https://badge.fury.io/py/httpx.svg" alt="Package version">
    16	</a>
    17	</p>
    18	
    19	HTTPX is an asynchronous HTTP client, that supports HTTP/2 and HTTP/1.1.
    20	
    21	It can be used in high-performance async web frameworks, using either asyncio
    22	or trio, and is able to support making large numbers of requests concurrently.
    23	
    24	**Note**: *HTTPX should still be considered in alpha. We'd love early users and feedback,
    25	but would strongly recommend pinning your dependencies to the latest median
    26	release, so that you're able to properly review API changes between package
    27	updates. Currently you should be using `httpx==0.9.*`.*
    28	
    29	*In particular, the 0.8 release switched HTTPX into focusing exclusively on
    30	providing an async client, in order to move the project forward, and help
    31	us [change our approach to providing sync+async support][sync-support]. If
    32	you have been using the sync client, you may want to pin to `httpx==0.7.*`,
    33	and wait until our sync client is reintroduced.*
    34	
    35	---
    36	
    37	Let's get started...
    38	
    39	*The standard Python REPL does not allow top-level async statements.*
    40	
    41	*To run async examples directly you'll probably want to either use `ipython`,
    42	or use Python 3.8 with `python -m asyncio`.*
    43	
    44	```python
    45	>>> import httpx
    46	>>> r = await httpx.get('https://www.example.org/')
    47	>>> r
    48	<Response [200 OK]>
    49	>>> r.status_code
    50	200
    51	>>> r.http_version
    52	'HTTP/1.1'
    53	>>> r.headers['content-type']
    54	'text/html; charset=UTF-8'
    55	>>> r.text
    56	'<!doctype html>\n<html>\n<head>\n<title>Example Domain</title>...'
    57	```
    58	
    59	## Features
    60	
    61	HTTPX builds on the well-established usability of `requests`, and gives you:
    62	
    63	* A requests-compatible API wherever possible.
    64	* HTTP/2 and HTTP/1.1 support.
    65	* Ability to [make requests directly to ASGI applications](https://www.encode.io/httpx/advanced/#calling-into-python-web-apps).
    66	* Strict timeouts everywhere.
    67	* Fully type annotated.
    68	* 100% test coverage.
    69	
    70	Plus all the standard features of `requests`...
    71	
    72	* International Domains and URLs
    73	* Keep-Alive & Connection Pooling
    74	* Sessions with Cookie Persistence
    75	* Browser-style SSL Verification
    76	* Basic/Digest Authentication
    77	* Elegant Key/Value Cookies
    78	* Automatic Decompression
    79	* Automatic Content Decoding
    80	* Unicode Response Bodies
    81	* Multipart File Uploads
    82	* HTTP(S) Proxy Support
    83	* Connection Timeouts
    84	* Streaming Downloads
    85	* .netrc Support
    86	* Chunked Requests
    87	
    88	## Installation
    89	
    90	Install with pip:
    91	
    92	```shell
    93	$ pip install httpx
    94	```
    95	
    96	httpx requires Python 3.6+
    97	
    98	## Documentation
    99	
   100	Project documentation is available at [www.encode.io/httpx/](https://www.encode.io/httpx/).
   101	
   102	For a run-through of all the basics, head over to the [QuickStart](https://www.encode.io/httpx/quickstart/).
   103	
   104	For more advanced topics, see the [Advanced Usage](https://www.encode.io/httpx/advanced/) section.
   105	
   106	The [Developer Interface](https://www.encode.io/httpx/api/) provides a comprehensive API reference.
   107	
   108	## Contribute
   109	
   110	If you want to contribute with HTTPX check out the [Contributing Guide](https://www.encode.io/httpx/contributing/) to learn how to start.
   111	
   112	## Dependencies
   113	
   114	The httpx project relies on these excellent libraries:
   115	
   116	* `h2` - HTTP/2 support.
   117	* `h11` - HTTP/1.1 support.
   118	* `certifi` - SSL certificates.
   119	* `chardet` - Fallback auto-detection for response encoding.
   120	* `hstspreload` - determines whether IDNA-encoded host should be only accessed via HTTPS.
   121	* `idna` - Internationalized domain name support.
   122	* `rfc3986` - URL parsing & normalization.
   123	* `sniffio` - Async library autodetection.
   124	* `brotlipy` - Decoding for "brotli" compressed responses. *(Optional)*
   125	
   126	A huge amount of credit is due to `requests` for the API layout that
   127	much of this work follows, as well as to `urllib3` for plenty of design
   128	inspiration around the lower-level networking details.
   129

     7	from ..config import Timeout
     8	from ..exceptions import ConnectTimeout, PoolTimeout, ReadTimeout, WriteTimeout
     9	from .base import BaseEvent, BasePoolSemaphore, BaseSocketStream, ConcurrencyBackend
    10	
    11	
    12	def none_as_inf(value: typing.Optional[float]) -> float:
    13	    return value if value is not None else float("inf")
    14	
    15	
    16	class SocketStream(BaseSocketStream):
    17	    def __init__(
    18	        self, stream: typing.Union[trio.SocketStream, trio.SSLStream],
    19	    ) -> None:
    20	        self.stream = stream
    21	        self.read_lock = trio.Lock()
    22	        self.write_lock = trio.Lock()
    23	
    24	    async def start_tls(
    25	        self, hostname: str, ssl_context: ssl.SSLContext, timeout: Timeout
    26	    ) -> "SocketStream":
    27	        connect_timeout = none_as_inf(timeout.connect_timeout)
    28	        ssl_stream = trio.SSLStream(
    29	            self.stream, ssl_context=ssl_context, server_hostname=hostname
    30	        )
    31	
    32	        with trio.move_on_after(connect_timeout):
    33	            await ssl_stream.do_handshake()
    34	            return SocketStream(ssl_stream)
    35	
    36	        raise ConnectTimeout()
    37	
    38	    def get_http_version(self) -> str:
    39	        if not isinstance(self.stream, trio.SSLStream):
    40	            return "HTTP/1.1"
    41	
    42	        ident = self.stream.selected_alpn_protocol()
    43	        return "HTTP/2" if ident == "h2" else "HTTP/1.1"
    44	
    45	    async def read(self, n: int, timeout: Timeout) -> bytes:
    46	        read_timeout = none_as_inf(timeout.read_timeout)
    47	
    48	        with trio.move_on_after(read_timeout):
    49	            async with self.read_lock:
    50	                return await self.stream.receive_some(max_bytes=n)
    51	
    52	        raise ReadTimeout()
    53	
    54	    async def write(self, data: bytes, timeout: Timeout) -> None:
    55	        if not data:
    56	            return
    57	
    58	        write_timeout = none_as_inf(timeout.write_timeout)
    59	
    60	        with trio.move_on_after(write_timeout):
    61	            async with self.write_lock:
    62	                return await self.stream.send_all(data)
    63	
    64	        raise WriteTimeout()
    65	
    66	    def is_connection_dropped(self) -> bool:
    67	        # Adapted from: https://github.com/encode/httpx/pull/143#issuecomment-515202982
    68	        stream = self.stream
    69	
    70	        # Peek through any SSLStream wrappers to get the underlying SocketStream.
    71	        while hasattr(stream, "transport_stream"):
    72	            stream = stream.transport_stream
    73	        assert isinstance(stream, trio.SocketStream)
    74	
    75	        # Counter-intuitively, what we really want to know here is whether the socket is
    76	        # *readable*, i.e. whether it would return immediately with empty bytes if we
    77	        # called `.recv()` on it, indicating that the other end has closed the socket.
    78	        # See: https://github.com/encode/httpx/pull/143#issuecomment-515181778
    79	        return stream.socket.is_readable()
    80	
    81	    async def close(self) -> None:
    82	        await self.stream.aclose()
    83	
    84	
    85	class PoolSemaphore(BasePoolSemaphore):
    86	    def __init__(self, max_value: int):
    87	        self.max_value = max_value
    88	
    89	    @property
    90	    def semaphore(self) -> trio.Semaphore:
    91	        if not hasattr(self, "_semaphore"):
    92	            self._semaphore = trio.Semaphore(self.max_value, max_value=self.max_value)
    93	        return self._semaphore
    94	
    95	    async def acquire(self, timeout: float = None) -> None:
    96	        timeout = none_as_inf(timeout)
    97	
    98	        with trio.move_on_after(timeout):
    99	            await self.semaphore.acquire()
   100	            return
   101	
   102	        raise PoolTimeout()
   103	
   104	    def release(self) -> None:
   105	        self.semaphore.release()
   106	
   107	
   108	class TrioBackend(ConcurrencyBackend):
   109	    async def open_tcp_stream(
   110	        self,
   111	        hostname: str,
   112	        port: int,
   113	        ssl_context: typing.Optional[ssl.SSLContext],
   114	        timeout: Timeout,
   115	    ) -> SocketStream:
   116	        connect_timeout = none_as_inf(timeout.connect_timeout)
   117	
   118	        with trio.move_on_after(connect_timeout):
   119	            stream: trio.SocketStream = await trio.open_tcp_stream(hostname, port)
   120	            if ssl_context is not None:
   121	                stream = trio.SSLStream(stream, ssl_context, server_hostname=hostname)
   122	                await stream.do_handshake()
   123	            return SocketStream(stream=stream)
   124	
   125	        raise ConnectTimeout()
   126	
   127	    async def open_uds_stream(
   128	        self,
   129	        path: str,
   130	        hostname: typing.Optional[str],
   131	        ssl_context: typing.Optional[ssl.SSLContext],
   132	        timeout: Timeout,
   133	    ) -> SocketStream:
   134	        connect_timeout = none_as_inf(timeout.connect_timeout)
   135	
   136	        with trio.move_on_after(connect_timeout):
   137	            stream: trio.SocketStream = await trio.open_unix_socket(path)
   138	            if ssl_context is not None:
   139	                stream = trio.SSLStream(stream, ssl_context, server_hostname=hostname)
   140	                await stream.do_handshake()
   141	            return SocketStream(stream=stream)
   142	
   143	        raise ConnectTimeout()
   144	
   145	    async def run_in_threadpool(
   146	        self, func: typing.Callable, *args: typing.Any, **kwargs: typing.Any
   147	    ) -> typing.Any:
   148	        return await trio.to_thread.run_sync(
   149	            functools.partial(func, **kwargs) if kwargs else func, *args
   150	        )
   151	
   152	    def run(
   153	        self, coroutine: typing.Callable, *args: typing.Any, **kwargs: typing.Any
   154	    ) -> typing.Any:
   155	        return trio.run(
   156	            functools.partial(coroutine, **kwargs) if kwargs else coroutine, *args
   157	        )
   158	
   159	    def time(self) -> float:
   160	        return trio.current_time()
   161	
   162	    def get_semaphore(self, max_value: int) -> BasePoolSemaphore:
   163	        return PoolSemaphore(max_value)

   165	    def create_event(self) -> BaseEvent:
   166	        return Event()

   173	    def set(self) -> None:
   174	        self._event.set()

   176	    async def wait(self) -> None:

     1	import functools
     2	import ssl
     3	import typing
     4	
     5	import h11
     6	
     7	from ..concurrency.base import BaseSocketStream, ConcurrencyBackend, lookup_backend
     8	from ..config import CertTypes, SSLConfig, Timeout, VerifyTypes
     9	from ..models import URL, Origin, Request, Response
    10	from ..utils import get_logger
    11	from .base import Dispatcher, OpenConnection
    12	from .http2 import HTTP2Connection
    13	from .http11 import HTTP11Connection
    14	
    15	# Callback signature: async def callback(conn: HTTPConnection) -> None
    16	ReleaseCallback = typing.Callable[["HTTPConnection"], typing.Awaitable[None]]
    17	
    18	
    19	logger = get_logger(__name__)
    20	
    21	
    22	class HTTPConnection(Dispatcher):
    23	    def __init__(
    24	        self,
    25	        origin: typing.Union[str, Origin],
    26	        verify: VerifyTypes = True,
    27	        cert: CertTypes = None,
    28	        trust_env: bool = None,
    29	        http2: bool = False,
    30	        backend: typing.Union[str, ConcurrencyBackend] = "auto",
    31	        release_func: typing.Optional[ReleaseCallback] = None,
    32	        uds: typing.Optional[str] = None,
    33	    ):
    34	        self.origin = Origin(origin) if isinstance(origin, str) else origin
    35	        self.ssl = SSLConfig(cert=cert, verify=verify, trust_env=trust_env)
    36	        self.http2 = http2
    37	        self.backend = lookup_backend(backend)
    38	        self.release_func = release_func
    39	        self.uds = uds
    40	        self.open_connection: typing.Optional[OpenConnection] = None
    41	        self.expires_at: typing.Optional[float] = None
    42	
    43	    async def send(
    44	        self,
    45	        request: Request,
    46	        verify: VerifyTypes = None,
    47	        cert: CertTypes = None,
    48	        timeout: Timeout = None,
    49	    ) -> Response:
    50	        timeout = Timeout() if timeout is None else timeout
    51	
    52	        if self.open_connection is None:
    53	            await self.connect(verify=verify, cert=cert, timeout=timeout)
    54	
    55	        assert self.open_connection is not None
    56	        response = await self.open_connection.send(request, timeout=timeout)
    57	
    58	        return response
    59	
    60	    async def connect(
    61	        self, timeout: Timeout, verify: VerifyTypes = None, cert: CertTypes = None,
    62	    ) -> None:
    63	        ssl = self.ssl.with_overrides(verify=verify, cert=cert)
    64	
    65	        host = self.origin.host
    66	        port = self.origin.port
    67	        ssl_context = await self.get_ssl_context(ssl)
    68	
    69	        if self.release_func is None:
    70	            on_release = None
    71	        else:
    72	            on_release = functools.partial(self.release_func, self)
    73	
    74	        if self.uds is None:
    75	            logger.trace(
    76	                f"start_connect tcp host={host!r} port={port!r} timeout={timeout!r}"
    77	            )
    78	            stream = await self.backend.open_tcp_stream(
    79	                host, port, ssl_context, timeout
    80	            )
    81	        else:
    82	            logger.trace(
    83	                f"start_connect uds path={self.uds!r} host={host!r} timeout={timeout!r}"
    84	            )
    85	            stream = await self.backend.open_uds_stream(
    86	                self.uds, host, ssl_context, timeout
    87	            )
    88	
    89	        http_version = stream.get_http_version()
    90	        logger.trace(f"connected http_version={http_version!r}")
    91	
    92	        self.set_open_connection(http_version, socket=stream, on_release=on_release)
    93	
    94	    async def tunnel_start_tls(
    95	        self,
    96	        origin: Origin,
    97	        proxy_url: URL,
    98	        timeout: Timeout = None,
    99	        cert: CertTypes = None,
   100	        verify: VerifyTypes = True,
   101	    ) -> None:
   102	        """
   103	        Upgrade this connection to use TLS, assuming it represents a TCP tunnel.
   104	        """
   105	        timeout = Timeout() if timeout is None else timeout
   106	
   107	        # First, check that we are in the correct state to start TLS, i.e. we've
   108	        # just agreed to switch protocols with the server via HTTP/1.1.
   109	        assert isinstance(self.open_connection, HTTP11Connection)
   110	        h11_connection = self.open_connection
   111	        assert h11_connection is not None
   112	        assert h11_connection.h11_state.our_state == h11.SWITCHED_PROTOCOL
   113	
   114	        # Store this information here so that we can transfer
   115	        # it to the new internal connection object after
   116	        # the old one goes to 'SWITCHED_PROTOCOL'.
   117	        # Note that the negotiated 'http_version' may change after the TLS upgrade.
   118	        http_version = "HTTP/1.1"
   119	        socket = h11_connection.socket
   120	        on_release = h11_connection.on_release
   121	
   122	        if origin.is_ssl:
   123	            # Pull the socket stream off the internal HTTP connection object,
   124	            # and run start_tls().
   125	            ssl_config = SSLConfig(cert=cert, verify=verify)
   126	            ssl_context = await self.get_ssl_context(ssl_config)
   127	            assert ssl_context is not None
   128	
   129	            logger.trace(f"tunnel_start_tls proxy_url={proxy_url!r} origin={origin!r}")
   130	            socket = await socket.start_tls(
   131	                hostname=origin.host, ssl_context=ssl_context, timeout=timeout
   132	            )
   133	            http_version = socket.get_http_version()
   134	            logger.trace(
   135	                f"tunnel_tls_complete "
   136	                f"proxy_url={proxy_url!r} "
   137	                f"origin={origin!r} "
   138	                f"http_version={http_version!r}"
   139	            )
   140	        else:
   141	            # User requested the use of a tunnel, but they're performing a plain-text
   142	            # HTTP request. Don't try to upgrade to TLS in this case.
   143	            pass
   144	
   145	        self.set_open_connection(http_version, socket=socket, on_release=on_release)
   146	
   147	    def set_open_connection(
   148	        self,
   149	        http_version: str,
   150	        socket: BaseSocketStream,
   151	        on_release: typing.Optional[typing.Callable],
   152	    ) -> None:
   153	        if http_version == "HTTP/2":
   154	            self.open_connection = HTTP2Connection(
   155	                socket, self.backend, on_release=on_release
   156	            )
   157	        else:
   158	            assert http_version == "HTTP/1.1"
   159	            self.open_connection = HTTP11Connection(socket, on_release=on_release)
   160	
   161	    async def get_ssl_context(self, ssl: SSLConfig) -> typing.Optional[ssl.SSLContext]:
   162	        if not self.origin.is_ssl:
   163	            return None
   164	
   165	        # Run the SSL loading in a threadpool, since it may make disk accesses.
   166	        return await self.backend.run_in_threadpool(ssl.load_ssl_context, self.http2)
   167	
   168	    async def close(self) -> None:
   169	        logger.trace("close_connection")
   170	        if self.open_connection is not None:
   171	            await self.open_connection.close()
   172	
   173	    @property
   174	    def is_http2(self) -> bool:
   175	        assert self.open_connection is not None
   176	        return self.open_connection.is_http2
   177	
   178	    @property
   179	    def is_closed(self) -> bool:
   180	        assert self.open_connection is not None
   181	        return self.open_connection.is_closed
   182	
   183	    def is_connection_dropped(self) -> bool:
   184	        assert self.open_connection is not None
   185	        return self.open_connection.is_connection_dropped()
   186	
   187	    def __repr__(self) -> str:
   188	        class_name = self.__class__.__name__
   189	        return f"{class_name}(origin={self.origin!r})"
   190

   445	    async def send_handling_redirects(
   446	        self,
   447	        request: Request,
   448	        auth: Auth,
   449	        timeout: Timeout,
   450	        verify: VerifyTypes = None,
   451	        cert: CertTypes = None,
   452	        allow_redirects: bool = True,
   453	        history: typing.List[Response] = None,
   454	    ) -> Response:
   455	        if history is None:
   456	            history = []
   457	
   458	        while True:
   459	            if len(history) > self.max_redirects:
   460	                raise TooManyRedirects()
   461	            if request.url in (response.url for response in history):
   462	                raise RedirectLoop()
   463	
   464	            response = await self.send_handling_auth(
   465	                request, auth=auth, timeout=timeout, verify=verify, cert=cert
   466	            )
   467	            response.history = list(history)
   468	
   469	            if not response.is_redirect:
   470	                return response
   471	
   472	            await response.read()
   473	            request = self.build_redirect_request(request, response)
   474	            history = history + [response]
   475	
   476	            if not allow_redirects:
   477	                response.call_next = functools.partial(
   478	                    self.send_handling_redirects,
   479	                    request=request,
   480	                    auth=auth,
   481	                    verify=verify,
   482	                    cert=cert,
   483	                    timeout=timeout,
   484	                    allow_redirects=False,
   485	                    history=history,
   486	                )
   487	                return response
   488	
   489	    def build_redirect_request(self, request: Request, response: Response) -> Request:
   490	        """
   491	        Given a request and a redirect response, return a new request that
   492	        should be used to effect the redirect.
   493	        """
   494	        method = self.redirect_method(request, response)
   495	        url = self.redirect_url(request, response)
   496	        headers = self.redirect_headers(request, url, method)
   497	        content = self.redirect_content(request, method)
   498	        cookies = Cookies(self.cookies)

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now let me think about the problem. The issue occurs when:

1. A request is made to an IP on port 80 (HTTP)
2. The server redirects to port 443 (HTTPS)
3. During the redirect, SSL handshake fails with WRONG_VERSION_NUMBER<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Looking at the code flow:

1. Initial request to HTTP (port 80) - this creates an HTTPConnection with origin.is_ssl = False
2. Server responds with redirect to HTTPS (port 443)  
3. Client builds redirect request with new URL that has https scheme
4. New request gets sent through the same connection pool/dispatch mechanism
5. The HTTPConnection needs to handle the transition from HTTP to HTTPS

The key issue is likely in the HTTPConnection.connect() method. When the origin changes from HTTP to HTTPS, the connection needs to establish a new SSL context and perform TLS handshake.

Looking at the trio.py file, I can see that the TrioBackend.open_tcp_stream method:
- Creates a plain TCP stream first
- If ssl_context is provided, wraps it in SSLStream and performs handshake

But there might be an issue when transitioning from a non-SSL connection to an SSL connection within the same connection lifecycle.

The problem might be that when we reuse an HTTPConnection that was initially created for HTTP (non-SSL), and then try to redirect to HTTPS, the connection isn't properly handling the SSL upgrade.

import trio
import httpx

from httpx.config import Timeout


async def test_connection_reuse_issue():
    """Test the specific issue with connection reuse during HTTP to HTTPS redirects."""
    
    print("Testing potential connection reuse issue...")
    
    # Create an HTTP connection
    origin_http = Origin("http://example.com:80")
    connection_http = HTTPConnection(origin_http, backend="trio")
    
    print(f"HTTP Origin: {origin_http}")
    print(f"Is SSL: {origin_http.is_ssl}")
    
    # Create an HTTPS connection  
    origin_https = Origin("https://example.com:443")
    connection_https = HTTPConnection(origin_https, backend="trio")
    
    print(f"HTTPS Origin: {origin_https}")
    print(f"Is SSL: {origin_https.is_ssl}")
    
    # Check if origins are equal (they shouldn't be)
    print(f"Origins equal: {origin_http == origin_https}")
    
    # Test with a real server that definitely redirects
    try:
        backend = TrioBackend()
        client = httpx.Client(backend=backend)
        
        # Try a server that might exhibit the issue
        # Let's use a server that we know has strict SSL requirements
        response = await client.get("http://httpbin.org/redirect-to?url=https%3A//httpbin.org/get", allow_redirects=True)
        print(f"Redirect success: {response.status_code}")
        print(f"Final URL: {response.url}")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()

        traceback.print_exc()

            await http_connection.close()
        except:
            pass

stream = <httpx.concurrency.asyncio.SocketStream object at 0x710060887880>, timeout = Timeout(timeout=5), should_contain = b'Hello, world'

    async def read_response(stream, timeout: Timeout, should_contain: bytes) -> bytes:
        # stream.read() only gives us *up to* as much data as we ask for. In order to
        # cleanly close the stream, we must read until the end of the HTTP response.
        response = b""
        ended = False

        for _ in range(5):  # Try read some (not too large) number of times...
            response += await stream.read(8192, timeout)
            # We know we're at the end of the response when we've received the body plus
            # the terminating CRLFs.
            if should_contain in response and response.endswith(b"\r\n\r\n"):
                ended = True
                break

>       assert ended

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>This test is doing:
1. Opening a TCP stream without SSL context (plain TCP)
2. Then calling `start_tls()` to upgrade it to SSL
3. Writing an HTTP request and reading the response

This is exactly the kind of scenario that could lead to the SSL WRONG_VERSION_NUMBER error if there's an issue with the TLS upgrade process.

    async def read_response(stream, timeout: Timeout, should_contain: bytes) -> bytes:
        # stream.read() only gives us *up to* as much data as we ask for. In order to
        # cleanly close the stream, we must read until the end of the HTTP response.
        response = b""
        ended = False

        for _ in range(5):  # Try read some (not too large) number of times...
            response += await stream.read(8192, timeout)
            # We know we're at the end of the response when we've received the body plus
            # the terminating CRLFs.
            if should_contain in response and response.endswith(b"\r\n\r\n"):
                ended = True
                break

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me analyze what I've learned:

1. The issue description mentions that the error occurs with httpx + trio when requesting an IP on port 80 that redirects to 443.

2. The error is: `ssl.SSLError: [SSL: WRONG_VERSION_NUMBER] wrong version number (_ssl.c:852)` occurring in trio's SSL implementation.

3. The failing tests show that both asyncio and trio backends have issues with the `start_tls` functionality - they can't properly upgrade a plain TCP connection to SSL.

4. Looking at the trio backend's `start_tls` method, it creates a new `trio.SSLStream` wrapping the existing stream and performs handshake.

5. The SSL WRONG_VERSION_NUMBER error typically occurs when:
   - You try to do SSL handshake on a connection that's already been used for non-SSL traffic
   - There's buffered data on the connection that interferes with the handshake
   - The SSL protocol version is incompatible

The root cause is likely that when `start_tls` is called, there might be leftover data in buffers or the connection state is not clean enough for the SSL handshake to succeed.

Looking at the trio backend code:
```python
async def start_tls(
    self, hostname: str, ssl_context: ssl.SSLContext, timeout: Timeout
) -> "SocketStream":
    connect_timeout = none_as_inf(timeout.connect_timeout)
    ssl_stream = trio.SSLStream(
        self.stream, ssl_context=ssl_context, server_hostname=hostname
    )

    with trio.move_on_after(connect_timeout):
        await ssl_stream.do_handshake()
        return SocketStream(ssl_stream)

    raise ConnectTimeout()
```

The issue might be that `self.stream` (the underlying trio.SocketStream) might have some state that interferes with the SSL handshake when wrapped in SSLStream.

async def reproduce_ssl_issue():
    """Try to reproduce the exact SSL WRONG_VERSION_NUMBER issue."""

    timeout = Timeout(10)

    # Let's try connecting to a plain HTTP server and then trying to upgrade to SSL

        # Step 1: Connect to a plain HTTP server

        stream = await backend.open_tcp_stream("httpbin.org", 80, None, timeout)
        print("Connected to HTTP server")

```
ssl.SSLError: [SSL: WRONG_VERSION_NUMBER] wrong version number (_ssl.c:1149)
```

This is happening in the trio backend's `start_tls` method when trying to upgrade a connection that has already been used for HTTP traffic.

The root cause is clear now:

**Problem Analysis:**
1. When a connection is established to a plain HTTP server, data is exchanged
2. When `start_tls()` is called to upgrade this connection to SSL, trio tries to do an SSL handshake on a connection that already has HTTP data in its buffers
3. This confuses the SSL protocol, leading to the WRONG_VERSION_NUMBER error

**Solution Approach:**
The trio backend needs to handle the TLS upgrade more carefully. Looking at the asyncio implementation, it uses the proper `loop.start_tls()` method which handles the upgrade correctly.

However, since trio doesn't have a direct equivalent, we need to ensure that:
1. Any buffered data is cleared before attempting SSL handshake
2. Or we create a fresh connection for the SSL upgrade

Let me examine the trio SSL documentation to see if there's a proper way to handle this.

1. There might be buffered data 
2. The SSL handshake expects a clean connection

Looking at this more carefully, I think the issue is that when we try to wrap an existing stream that has been used for HTTP communication in an SSLStream, we're essentially trying to do SSL on top of data that's already been transmitted.

The proper approach would be to either:
1. Ensure the stream is clean before wrapping it (flush any buffers)
2. Create a fresh connection for the SSL communication

Looking at the failing tests, they're doing:
1. Open TCP stream without SSL
2. Call start_tls to upgrade it
3. Try to read/write HTTP data

The issue is in step 2. Let me check if there's a way to flush the stream or if we need to approach this differently.

Actually, let me look at this from another angle. The issue might be that we're trying to do SSL handshake on a stream that has already had data written to it. Let me check if we can detect this and handle it appropriately.

Looking at the trio backend code again:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/encode__httpx__0.9/httpx/concurrency/trio.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>view</arg_value><arg_key>view_range</arg_key><arg_value>[16, 82]</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Here's the result of running `cat -n` on /workspace/encode__httpx__0.9/httpx/concurrency/trio.py:
    16	class SocketStream(BaseSocketStream):
    17	    def __init__(
    18	        self, stream: typing.Union[trio.SocketStream, trio.SSLStream],
    19	    ) -> None:
    20	        self.stream = stream
    21	        self.read_lock = trio.Lock()
    22	        self.write_lock = trio.Lock()
    23	
    24	    async def start_tls(
    25	        self, hostname: str, ssl_context: ssl.SSLContext, timeout: Timeout
    26	    ) -> "SocketStream":
    27	        connect_timeout = none_as_inf(timeout.connect_timeout)
    28	        ssl_stream = trio.SSLStream(
    29	            self.stream, ssl_context=ssl_context, server_hostname=hostname
    30	        )
    31	
    32	        with trio.move_on_after(connect_timeout):
    33	            await ssl_stream.do_handshake()
    34	            return SocketStream(ssl_stream)
    35	
    36	        raise ConnectTimeout()
    37	
    38	    def get_http_version(self) -> str:
    39	        if not isinstance(self.stream, trio.SSLStream):
    40	            return "HTTP/1.1"
    41	
    42	        ident = self.stream.selected_alpn_protocol()
    43	        return "HTTP/2" if ident == "h2" else "HTTP/1.1"
    44	
    45	    async def read(self, n: int, timeout: Timeout) -> bytes:
    46	        read_timeout = none_as_inf(timeout.read_timeout)
    47	
    48	        with trio.move_on_after(read_timeout):
    49	            async with self.read_lock:
    50	                return await self.stream.receive_some(max_bytes=n)
    51	
    52	        raise ReadTimeout()
    53	
    54	    async def write(self, data: bytes, timeout: Timeout) -> None:
    55	        if not data:
    56	            return
    57	
    58	        write_timeout = none_as_inf(timeout.write_timeout)
    59	
    60	        with trio.move_on_after(write_timeout):
    61	            async with self.write_lock:
    62	                return await self.stream.send_all(data)
    63	
    64	        raise WriteTimeout()
    65	
    66	    def is_connection_dropped(self) -> bool:
    67	        # Adapted from: https://github.com/encode/httpx/pull/143#issuecomment-515202982
    68	        stream = self.stream
    69	
    70	        # Peek through any SSLStream wrappers to get the underlying SocketStream.
    71	        while hasattr(stream, "transport_stream"):
    72	            stream = stream.transport_stream
    73	        assert isinstance(stream, trio.SocketStream)
    74	
    75	        # Counter-intuitively, what we really want to know here is whether the socket is
    76	        # *readable*, i.e. whether it would return immediately with empty bytes if we
    77	        # called `.recv()` on it, indicating that the other end has closed the socket.
    78	        # See: https://github.com/encode/httpx/pull/143#issuecomment-515181778
    79	        return stream.socket.is_readable()
    80	
    81	    async def close(self) -> None:
    82	        await self.stream.aclose()

"""Debug script to understand stream state."""

import trio
from httpx.concurrency.trio import TrioBackend
from httpx.config import Timeout


async def debug_stream_state():
    """Debug the state of streams before and after usage."""
    
    backend = TrioBackend()
    timeout = Timeout(5)
    
    # Create a plain TCP stream
    print("Creating plain TCP stream...")
    stream = await backend.open_tcp_stream("httpbin.org", 80, None, timeout)
    print(f"Stream type: {type(stream.stream)}")
    print(f"Stream attributes: {[attr for attr in dir(stream.stream) if not attr.startswith('_')]}")
    
    # Check if we can detect if it's been used
    if hasattr(stream.stream, 'socket'):
        print(f"Socket: {stream.stream.socket}")
        print(f"Socket type: {type(stream.stream.socket)}")
    
    # Write some data
    print("\nWriting HTTP request...")
    await stream.write(b"GET / HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: httpbin.org\r\n\r\n", timeout)
    print("Write completed")
    
    # Try to read some response
    try:
        print("\nReading response...")
        data = await stream.read(1024, timeout)
        print(f"Read {len(data)} bytes")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Read error: {e}")
    
    # Check stream state after usage
    print(f"\nStream after usage - type: {type(stream.stream)}")
    if hasattr(stream.stream, 'socket'):
        print(f"Socket after usage: {stream.stream.socket}")

What if I modify the `start_tls` method to recreate the underlying connection?<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/encode__httpx__0.9/experimental_fix.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Experiment with different approaches to fix start_tls."""

import trio
import ssl
from httpx.concurrency.trio import TrioBackend, SocketStream
from httpx.config import Timeout, SSLConfig


# Experimental fix - try to create a fresh connection instead of upgrading
async def experimental_start_tls(self, hostname: str, ssl_context: ssl.SSLContext, timeout: Timeout) -> "SocketStream":
    """Experimental start_tls that creates a fresh connection."""
    print("Using experimental start_tls")
    
    # Get the remote address from the current stream
    if hasattr(self.stream, 'socket') and hasattr(self.stream.socket, 'getpeername'):
        remote_addr = self.stream.socket.getpeername()
        host, port = remote_addr[0], remote_addr[1]
        print(f"Reconnecting to {host}:{port}")
        
        # Close the existing stream
        try:
            await self.stream.aclose()
        except:
            pass
            
        # Create a fresh connection with SSL
        connect_timeout = TrioBackend.none_as_inf(timeout.connect_timeout)
        with trio.move_on_after(connect_timeout):
            new_stream = await trio.open_tcp_stream(host, port)
            ssl_stream = trio.SSLStream(new_stream, ssl_context, server_hostname=hostname)
            await ssl_stream.do_handshake()
            return SocketStream(ssl_stream)
    
    # Fall back to original method if we can't get address info
    print("Falling back to original method")
    connect_timeout = TrioBackend.none_as_inf(timeout.connect_timeout)
    ssl_stream = trio.SSLStream(
        self.stream, ssl_context=ssl_context, server_hostname=hostname
    )

    with trio.move_on_after(connect_timeout):
        await ssl_stream.do_handshake()
        return SocketStream(ssl_stream)

    raise Exception("Connect timeout")


async def test_experimental_fix():
    """Test the experimental fix."""
    
    backend = TrioBackend()
    timeout = Timeout(10)
    
    try:
        # Connect to HTTP endpoint
        print("Connecting to HTTP endpoint")
        stream = await backend.open_tcp_stream("httpbin.org", 80, None, timeout)
        print("Connected")
        
        # Write some data
        await stream.write(b"GET / HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: httpbin.org\r\n\r\n", timeout)
        print("Wrote HTTP request")
        
        # Try to read some response
        try:
            data = await stream.read(1024, timeout)
            print(f"Read {len(data)} bytes")
        except:
            print("No data read")
            
        # Apply our monkey patch
        original_start_tls = stream.start_tls
        stream.start_tls = experimental_start_tls.__get__(stream, SocketStream)
        
        # Try SSL upgrade
        print("Attempting SSL upgrade with experimental fix")
        ssl_config = SSLConfig()
        ssl_context = ssl_config.load_ssl_context_no_verify()
        
        upgraded_stream = await stream.start_tls("httpbin.org", ssl_context, timeout)
        print("SSL upgrade successful!")
        
        return True
        
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False

This should definitely work. The fact that it's failing suggests there's a real bug in the trio backend's `start_tls` implementation.

Let me think about what could be wrong. Maybe the issue is that when we wrap a plain `SocketStream` in `SSLStream`, something goes wrong.

Let me check if there are any differences in how trio's SSLStream should be used vs how we're using it.

Actually, let me look at trio's documentation or examples for SSLStream usage.

But first, let me try to understand what's different between the asyncio and trio implementations that might cause this.

The asyncio version:
1. Gets the transport from the StreamWriter
2. Uses `loop.start_tls()` to upgrade the transport
3. Creates new StreamReader/StreamWriter instances
4. Keeps a reference to the old stream to prevent garbage collection

The trio version:
1. Wraps `self.stream` directly in `trio.SSLStream`  
2. Calls `do_handshake()` on the SSLStream

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Clear Problem Statement

**Problem:** 
The trio backend's `start_tls` method fails with `ssl.SSLError: [SSL: WRONG_VERSION_NUMBER] wrong version number` when trying to upgrade a plain TCP connection to SSL.

**Location:** 
`/workspace/encode__httpx__0.9/httpx/concurrency/trio.py`, lines 24-36, in the `start_tls` method of the `SocketStream` class.

**How the test reproduces the issue:**
1. Connect to an HTTPS test server using `open_tcp_stream` with `ssl_context=None` (creates plain TCP)
2. Call `start_tls` to upgrade the connection to SSL
3. The SSL handshake fails with WRONG_VERSION_NUMBER error

**Root Cause Analysis:**
The SSL WRONG_VERSION_NUMBER error occurs when the SSL/TLS client receives data that doesn't conform to the expected SSL protocol. This typically happens when:
- Non-SSL data is sent to an SSL endpoint before the handshake
- The connection is in an inconsistent state
- There's buffered data interfering with the handshake

In our case, we're wrapping an existing `trio.SocketStream` (which may have internal buffering or state) directly in `trio.SSLStream` and immediately trying to do handshake.

**Best practices to consider:**
1. Ensure clean connection state before SSL handshake
2. Handle potential buffering issues  
3. Follow trio's recommended patterns for SSL usage
4. Maintain backward compatibility

**How to fix the problem:**
Looking at how the asyncio backend handles this correctly, the key difference is that it uses the proper `loop.start_tls()` method and creates new stream objects rather than wrapping the existing ones.

For trio, we should:
1. Ensure the underlying stream is in a clean state before SSL handshake
2. Or create a fresh connection for the SSL upgrade (similar to my experimental approach, but correctly)

Actually, let me reconsider the experimental approach. The issue was that I was trying to reconnect to the same port (80) instead of creating a proper SSL connection.

Let me look at this differently. The proper fix should be to ensure that when we do the SSL handshake, the underlying connection is clean.

    :class:`SSLStream` wraps an arbitrary :class:`~trio.abc.Stream`, and
    allows you to perform encrypted communication over it using the usual
    :class:`~trio.abc.Stream` interface. You pass regular data to
    :meth:`send_all`, then it encrypts it and sends the encrypted data on the
    underlying :class:`~trio.abc.Stream`; :meth:`receive_some` takes encrypted
    data out of the underlying :class:`~trio.abc.Stream` and decrypts it
    before returning it.

    You should read the standard library's :mod:`ssl` documentation carefully
    before attempting to use this class, and probably other general
    documentation on SSL/TLS as well. SSL/TLS is subtle and quick to
    anger. Really. I'm not kidding.

    Args:
      transport_stream (~trio.abc.Stream): The stream used to transport
          encrypted data. Required.

      ssl_context (~ssl.SSLContext): The :class:`~ssl.SSLContext` used for
          this connection. Required. Usually created by calling
          :func:`ssl.create_default_context`.

      server_hostname (str, bytes, or None): The name of the server being
          connected to. Used for `SNI
          <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Server_Name_Indication>`__ and for
          validating the server's certificate (if hostname checking is
          enabled). This is effectively mandatory for clients, and actually
          mandatory if ``ssl_context.check_hostname`` is ``True``.

      server_side (bool): Whether this stream is acting as a client or
          server. Defaults to False, i.e. client mode.

      https_compatible (bool): There are two versions of SSL/TLS commonly
          encountered in the wild: the standard version, and the version used
          for HTTPS (HTTP-over-SSL/TLS).

          Standard-compliant SSL/TLS implementations always send a
          cryptographically signed ``close_notify`` message before closing the
          connection. This is important because if the underlying transport
          were simply closed, then there wouldn't be any way for the other
          side to know whether the connection was intentionally closed by the
          peer that they negotiated a cryptographic connection to, or by some
          `man-in-the-middle
          <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Man-in-the-middle_attack>`__ attacker
          who can't manipulate the cryptographic stream, but can manipulate
          the transport layer (a so-called "truncation attack").

          However, this part of the standard is widely ignored by real-world
          HTTPS implementations, which means that if you want to interoperate

import inspect

"</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>async def open_ssl_over_tcp_stream(
    host: str | bytes,
    port: int,
    *,
    https_compatible: bool = False,
    ssl_context: ssl.SSLContext | None = None,
    happy_eyeballs_delay: float | None = DEFAULT_DELAY,
) -> trio.SSLStream[SocketStream]:
    """Make a TLS-encrypted Connection to the given host and port over TCP.

    This is a convenience wrapper that calls :func:`open_tcp_stream` and
    wraps the result in an :class:`~trio.SSLStream`.

    This function does not perform the TLS handshake; you can do it
    manually by calling :meth:`~trio.SSLStream.do_handshake`, or else
    it will be performed automatically the first time you send or receive
    data.

    Args:
      host (bytes or str): The host to connect to. We require the server
          to have a TLS certificate valid for this hostname.
      port (int): The port to connect to.
      https_compatible (bool): Set this to True if you're connecting to a web
          server. See :class:`~trio.SSLStream` for details. Default:
          False.
      ssl_context (:class:`~ssl.SSLContext` or None): The SSL context to
          use. If None (the default), :func:`ssl.create_default_context`
          will be called to create a context.
      happy_eyeballs_delay (float): See :func:`open_tcp_stream`.

    Returns:
      trio.SSLStream: the encrypted connection to the server.

    """
    tcp_stream = await trio.open_tcp_stream(
        host,
        port,
        happy_eyeballs_delay=happy_eyeballs_delay,
    )
    if ssl_context is None:
        ssl_context = ssl.create_default_context()

        if hasattr(ssl, "OP_IGNORE_UNEXPECTED_EOF"):
            ssl_context.options &= ~ssl.OP_IGNORE_UNEXPECTED_EOF

    return trio.SSLStream(
        tcp_stream,
        ssl_context,
        server_hostname=host,
        https_compatible=https_compatible,
    )

    87

    89	        protocol = asyncio.StreamReaderProtocol(stream_reader)
    90	        transport = self.stream_writer.transport

   106	            transport=transport, protocol=protocol, reader=stream_reader, loop=loop

   111	        # we need to keep references to the old StreamReader/StreamWriter so that they

   113	        ssl_stream._inner = self
   114	        return ssl_stream

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>The asyncio version:
1. Gets the existing transport from the StreamWriter
2. Uses `loop.start_tls()` to upgrade the transport to SSL
3. Creates new StreamReader/StreamWriter instances with the upgraded transport
4. Keeps a reference to the old stream to prevent premature cleanup

So the asyncio version is using the proper asyncio API to upgrade an existing transport to SSL.

async def test_trio_ssl_upgrade():
    """Test trio SSL upgrade like the failing test does."""
    
    print("Testing trio SSL upgrade...")
    
    # Step 1: Connect to localhost:8001 (our test HTTPS server) with plain TCP
    print("Connecting to test HTTPS server with plain TCP...")
    tcp_stream = await trio.open_tcp_stream("localhost", 8001)
    print(f"Connected: {tcp_stream}")
    
    # Step 2: Create SSL context (like the test does)
    print("Creating SSL context...")
    ssl_context = ssl.create_default_context()
    ssl_context.check_hostname = False
    ssl_context.verify_mode = ssl.CERT_NONE
    
    # Step 3: Wrap in SSLStream and try handshake (like start_tls does)
    print("Wrapping in SSLStream...")
    ssl_stream = trio.SSLStream(
        tcp_stream, 
        ssl_context, 
        server_hostname="localhost"
    )
    
    print("Attempting SSL handshake...")
    try:
        await ssl_stream.do_handshake()
        print("SUCCESS: SSL handshake completed")
    except ssl.SSLError as e:
        if "WRONG_VERSION_NUMBER" in str(e):
            print(f"REPRODUCED THE ISSUE: {e}")
            return True
        else:
            print(f"Different SSL error: {e}")

        print(f"Other error: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")

        self, hostname: str, ssl_context: ssl.SSLContext, timeout: Timeout
    ) -> "SocketStream":
        connect_timeout = none_as_inf(timeout.connect_timeout)
        
        # If the underlying stream has a socket, we can recreate a fresh connection
        if hasattr(self.stream, 'socket') and hasattr(self.stream.socket, 'getpeername'):
            try:
                # Get the remote address to reconnect to the same endpoint
                remote_addr = self.stream.socket.getpeername()
                host, port = remote_addr[0], remote_addr[1]
                
                # Close the existing stream
                await self.stream.aclose()
                
                # Create a fresh TCP connection and wrap it in SSLStream
                new_stream = await trio.open_tcp_stream(host, port)
                ssl_stream = trio.SSLStream(
                    new_stream, ssl_context=ssl_context, server_hostname=hostname
                )
                
                with trio.move_on_after(connect_timeout):
                    await ssl_stream.do_handshake()
                    return SocketStream(ssl_stream)
                    
            except Exception:
                # If we can't get address info or reconnect, fall back to original method
                pass
        
        # Original method as fallback
        ssl_stream = trio.SSLStream(
            self.stream, ssl_context=ssl_context, server_hostname=hostname
        )

        with trio.move_on_after(connect_timeout):
            await ssl_stream.do_handshake()
            return SocketStream(ssl_stream)

Let me create a better test that connects to a proper HTTPS endpoint first:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/encode__httpx__0.9/test_fix.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Test the fix with proper HTTPS server."""

import trio
import ssl
from httpx.concurrency.trio import TrioBackend
from httpx.config import Timeout, SSLConfig


async def test_fix_with_real_server():
    """Test the fix with a real HTTPS server."""
    
    print("Testing fix with real HTTPS server...")
    
    backend = TrioBackend()
    timeout = Timeout(10)
    
    try:
        # Connect to a real HTTPS server with plain TCP first
        print("Connecting to HTTPS server with plain TCP...")
        stream = await backend.open_tcp_stream("httpbin.org", 443, None, timeout)
        print("Connected to HTTPS server (plain TCP)")
        
        # Now try to upgrade to SSL - this should work with our fix
        print("Attempting SSL upgrade...")
        ssl_config = SSLConfig()
        ssl_context = ssl_config.load_ssl_context_no_verify()
        
        upgraded_stream = await stream.start_tls("httpbin.org", ssl_context, timeout)
        print("SUCCESS: SSL upgrade worked!")
        
        # Try to send an HTTP request
        await upgraded_stream.write(b"GET / HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: httpbin.org\r\n\r\n", timeout)
        response = await upgraded_stream.read(1024, timeout)
        print(f"Received {len(response)} bytes response")
        if b"HTTP/1.1" in response:
            print("SUCCESS: HTTP response received")
            return True
        else:
            print("ERROR: No valid HTTP response")
            
    except ssl.SSLError as e:
        if "WRONG_VERSION_NUMBER" in str(e):
            print(f"FAILED: Still getting WRONG_VERSION_NUMBER: {e}")
        else:
            print(f"Different SSL error: {e}")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Other error: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()
    
    return False


if __name__ == "__main__":

            data = await stream.read(8192, timeout)
            print(f"Read {len(data)} bytes")
            response += data
            print(f"Response so far: {response[:100]}...")
            
            # Check if we have the expected content and ending

            print("SUCCESS: Got expected response")
        else:
            print("ERROR: Unexpected response start")

        print(f"Error: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")

        traceback.print_exc()

        self, hostname: str, ssl_context: ssl.SSLContext, timeout: Timeout
    ) -> "SocketStream":
        connect_timeout = none_as_inf(timeout.connect_timeout)
        
        # If the underlying stream has a socket, we can recreate a fresh connection
        if hasattr(self.stream, 'socket') and hasattr(self.stream.socket, 'getpeername'):
            try:
                # Get the remote address to reconnect to the same endpoint
                remote_addr = self.stream.socket.getpeername()
                host, port = remote_addr[0], remote_addr[1]
                
                # Validate that port is an integer (not for Unix domain sockets)
                if isinstance(port, int):
                    # Close the existing stream
                    await self.stream.aclose()
                    
                    # Create a fresh TCP connection and wrap it in SSLStream
                    with trio.move_on_after(connect_timeout):
                        new_stream = await trio.open_tcp_stream(host, port)
                        ssl_stream = trio.SSLStream(
                            new_stream, ssl_context=ssl_context, server_hostname=hostname
                        )
                        await ssl_stream.do_handshake()
                        return SocketStream(ssl_stream)
